url: https://blog.vidocsecurity.com/blog/vibe-coding-security-vulnerabilities
title: "Vibe Coding Security: 9 Real Vulnerabilities in AI-Generated Code (SQLi, XSS, Broken Auth & More) - Vidoc Security Lab"
---

## What is \"vibe coding\" and why security teams care {#what-is-vibe-coding-and-why-security-teams-care .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

\"Vibe coding\" is the practice of building software primarily through natural-language prompts and AI code generation instead of writing every line by hand. It boosts delivery speed and lowers the barrier to entry, but the same speed and abstraction can hide security risk. Major vendors explicitly call out the need for a security review step in the AI-assisted dev loop ([Google Cloud](https://cloud.google.com/discover/what-is-vibe-coding "Vibe Coding Explained: Tools and Guides | Google Cloud"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [IBM](https://www.ibm.com/think/topics/vibe-coding "What is Vibe Coding? | IBM"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).

## The empirical risk {#the-empirical-risk .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Independent measurements consistently show high rates of insecure completions: Veracode reports that nearly half of AI-generated code tasks contain security issues (\~45%) ([Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250730694951/en/AI-Generated-Code-Poses-Major-Security-Risks-in-Nearly-Half-of-All-Development-Tasks-Veracode-Research-Reveals "AI-Generated Code Poses Major Security Risks in Nearly Half of All Development Tasks, Veracode Research Reveals"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}), and an earlier academic evaluation of GitHub Copilot found roughly 40% of generated programs were vulnerable across high-risk CWEs ([arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.09293 "[2108.09293] Asleep at the Keyboard? Assessing the Security of GitHub Copilot's Code Contributions"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}). These failures map directly to common OWASP categories: input validation (SQL injection, XSS), auth/authorization, insecure data handling, and dependency risks ([Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250730694951/en/AI-Generated-Code-Poses-Major-Security-Risks-in-Nearly-Half-of-All-Development-Tasks-Veracode-Research-Reveals "AI-Generated Code Poses Major Security Risks in Nearly Half of All Development Tasks, Veracode Research Reveals"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).

## Failure modes you'll actually see in PRs {#failure-modes-youll-actually-see-in-prs .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

-   **Input validation problems (SQLi, XSS)**: Unsanitized concatenated queries, improper HTML escaping, unsafe template rendering ([TechRepublic](https://www.techrepublic.com/article/news-anthropic-claude-code-ai-security-review/ "Anthropic’s Claude Code Arms Developers With Always-On AI Security Reviews"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [Snyk](https://snyk.io/blog/ai-code-generation-code-security-quality-benefits-risks-top-tools/ "AI Code Generation: Code Security & Quality, Benefits, Risks & Tools | Snyk"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **AuthN/AuthZ omissions**: Missing auth checks on new endpoints; role checks that can be bypassed ([Cybernews](https://cybernews.com/security/ai-generated-code-security-risks-outpace-cyber-professionals/ "AI-generated code is serving up serious security risks, say researchers | Cybernews"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [TechRepublic](https://www.techrepublic.com/article/news-anthropic-claude-code-ai-security-review/ "Anthropic’s Claude Code Arms Developers With Always-On AI Security Reviews"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **Secrets in code and config drift**: Hardcoded tokens, keys in `.env`{.[p_&]:text-sm .[p_&]:px-1 .[p_&]:py-0.5 .[p_&]:rounded-none .[p_&]:bg-gray-200 .dark:[p_&]:bg-[#333]} committed by accident, and drift between local and prod configs ([Software Mind](https://softwaremind.com/blog/the-rise-and-risk-of-vibe-coding-whats-worth-knowing/ "The Pros and Cons of Vibe Coding"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [GitGuardian](https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2025/ "The State of Secrets Sprawl 2025"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **Dangerous deserialization and memory-unsafe patterns**: In Python, `pickle`{.[p_&]:text-sm .[p_&]:px-1 .[p_&]:py-0.5 .[p_&]:rounded-none .[p_&]:bg-gray-200 .dark:[p_&]:bg-[#333]} loaders wired to untrusted input; in C/C++, buffer and integer overflows from unsafe APIs ([Databricks](https://www.databricks.com/blog/passing-security-vibe-check-dangers-vibe-coding "Passing the Security Vibe Check: The Dangers of Vibe Coding | Databricks Blog"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [heise online](https://www.heise.de/en/news/Databricks-Recognizing-and-avoiding-security-vulnerabilities-in-vibe-coding-10521686.html "Databricks: Recognizing and avoiding security vulnerabilities in vibe coding | heise online"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **Supply-chain via hallucinated packages (\"slopsquatting\")**: LLM suggests a non-existent dependency; an attacker registers it under that name to get code execution in your build or runtime ([TechRadar](https://www.techradar.com/pro/mitigating-the-risks-of-package-hallucination-and-slopsquatting "Mitigating the risks of package hallucination and 'slopsquatting' | TechRadar"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [SecurityWeek](https://www.securityweek.com/vibe-coding-when-everyones-a-developer-who-secures-the-code/ "Vibe Coding: When Everyone’s a Developer, Who Secures the Code? - SecurityWeek"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).

> **See it in practice:** We chained several of these vulnerabilities in a real-world AI app --- read [how we secured Lovable](/blog/how-we-secured-lovable){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white} for a full exploit walkthrough, or see our [API security best practices](/blog/api-security-best-practices-for-developers){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white} for more coverage of auth and injection issues.

## Common frontend vulnerabilities in AI-generated code {#common-frontend-vulnerabilities-in-ai-generated-code .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

AI code generation excels at creating functional interfaces quickly, but often misses critical security fundamentals that experienced developers take for granted. Let\'s explore the most common frontend security gaps you\'ll encounter.

### 1. Insecure HTTP connections {#1-insecure-http-connections .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Missing HTTPS enforcement** AI-generated applications frequently default to HTTP during development and fail to enforce HTTPS in production. This leaves all communication vulnerable to eavesdropping, man-in-the-middle attacks, and credential theft. Even worse, many developers deploy these applications without configuring proper TLS redirects or security headers.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Always enforce HTTPS in production and set proper security headers. Configure automatic redirects from HTTP to HTTPS, and implement HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS) to prevent protocol downgrade attacks.

**Other tips for preventing insecure connections:**

-   Use Let\'s Encrypt or cloud provider certificates for free TLS
-   Test your HTTPS configuration with [SSL Labs](https://www.ssllabs.com/ssltest/){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}
-   Never mix HTTP and HTTPS resources on the same page

### 2. Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) vulnerabilities {#2-cross-site-scripting-xss-vulnerabilities .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Insufficient input validation** AI-generated forms and search functionality often lack proper input validation and output encoding. This creates XSS vulnerabilities where malicious scripts can be injected and executed in other users\' browsers, potentially stealing credentials, session tokens, or performing actions on behalf of victims.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Implement comprehensive input validation on all user-provided data. Validate data types, formats, and ranges before processing. Use established validation libraries rather than rolling your own.

[`1`{style="white-space:pre;font-size:0.9rem;font-family:var(--font-mono)"}]{.linenumber .react-syntax-highlighter-line-number **Other tips for preventing XSS:**

-   Use Content Security Policy (CSP) headers to restrict script execution
-   Encode output appropriately for the context (HTML, JavaScript, CSS, URL)
-   Never insert user data directly into script tags or event handlers

### 3. Insecure client-side data storage {#3-insecure-client-side-data-storage .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Sensitive data in browser storage** AI-generated authentication flows commonly store JWT tokens, API keys, or user data in localStorage or sessionStorage. This data is accessible to any JavaScript running on the page, making it vulnerable to XSS attacks and browser extensions with malicious intent.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Use secure, HttpOnly cookies for authentication tokens. Store only non-sensitive data in browser storage, and ensure sensitive operations always validate server-side.

**Other tips for secure data storage:**

-   Never store passwords, tokens, or API keys in browser storage
-   Use secure cookie flags: HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite
-   Implement proper session management with server-side validation

## Critical backend vulnerabilities in vibe-coded applications {#critical-backend-vulnerabilities-in-vibe-coded-applications .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Backend security is where AI-generated code shows its most dangerous gaps. While AI excels at creating functional APIs, it consistently misses authorization checks, proper authentication, and secure data handling practices that prevent serious breaches.

### 4. Weak password storage and authentication {#4-weak-password-storage-and-authentication .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Insecure password handling** AI-generated authentication systems frequently use weak hashing algorithms like MD5 or SHA-1, or even store passwords in plain text. Some implement custom authentication schemes that bypass established security practices, creating easy targets for credential-based attacks.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Always use modern, robust password hashing algorithms designed for password storage. Argon2id is currently the gold standard, offering excellent protection against both online and offline attacks.

**Other tips for secure authentication:**

-   Never roll your own crypto - use established libraries
-   Implement account lockout after failed attempts
-   Require strong passwords and consider implementing MFA

### 5. Missing authorization checks {#5-missing-authorization-checks .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Broken function-level authorization** AI-generated API endpoints often assume that authentication equals authorization. They verify that a user is logged in but fail to check whether that specific user should be allowed to perform the requested action, leading to privilege escalation vulnerabilities.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Implement proper role-based access control (RBAC) that checks both authentication and authorization for every protected action. Always verify that the current user has permission to access the requested resource.

[`1`{style="white-space:pre;font-size:0.9rem;font-family:var(--font-mono)"}]{.linenumber .react-syntax-highlighter-line-number **Other tips for preventing authorization bypasses:**

-   Implement the principle of least privilege
-   Use deny-by-default policies (whitelist permissions)
-   Regularly audit user permissions and roles

### 6. SQL injection vulnerabilities {#6-sql-injection-vulnerabilities .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Dynamic SQL construction** AI-generated database queries often concatenate user input directly into SQL strings, creating classic SQL injection vulnerabilities. This happens most frequently in search functionality, filtering, and dynamic query construction.

[`1`{style="white-space:pre;font-size:0.9rem;font-family:var(--font-mono)"}]{.linenumber .react-syntax-highlighter-line-number ### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Always use parameterized queries or prepared statements that separate SQL logic from user data. This prevents injection attacks by ensuring user input is treated as data, never as executable code.

**Other tips for preventing SQL injection:**

-   Use ORM libraries that provide built-in parameterization
-   Never use dynamic SQL construction with user input
-   Apply input validation and sanitization before database operations
-   Use stored procedures where appropriate

## Operational security gaps in vibe-coded projects {#operational-security-gaps-in-vibe-coded-projects .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Beyond code-level vulnerabilities, AI-generated applications often miss crucial operational security practices. These gaps can turn minor issues into major breaches.

### 7. Information disclosure through error handling {#7-information-disclosure-through-error-handling .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Verbose error messages** AI-generated error handling frequently exposes sensitive information like database schema details, file paths, internal server configuration, or stack traces. This information helps attackers understand your system\'s architecture and identify additional attack vectors.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Implement proper error handling that logs detailed information server-side while returning generic, non-revealing messages to clients. Use error tracking services for debugging without exposing internals.

**Other tips for secure error handling:**

-   Never expose stack traces, SQL queries, or file paths in responses
-   Use centralized error handling middleware
-   Implement proper logging and monitoring for security events

### 8. Uncontrolled file uploads {#8-uncontrolled-file-uploads .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: Insufficient file validation** AI-generated file upload features often accept any file type and size, creating opportunities for malware uploads, storage exhaustion attacks, and potential code execution if files are served from the same domain.

### How to fix this vulnerability {#how-to-fix-this-vulnerability .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

Implement comprehensive file validation including type checking, size limits, content scanning, and secure storage practices. Never trust client-provided file information.

**Other tips for secure file uploads:**

-   Store files outside the web root or use cloud storage
-   Implement virus scanning for uploaded files
-   Use Content-Disposition headers to prevent execution
-   Set up proper access controls for stored files

### 9. Lack of security monitoring {#9-lack-of-security-monitoring .group .font-sans .text-[1.5em] .font-semibold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-10 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

**Vulnerability: No security event tracking** AI-generated applications rarely include security monitoring, logging of suspicious activities, or alerting mechanisms. This makes it difficult to detect attacks in progress or investigate security incidents.

**Other tips for security monitoring:**

-   Implement dependency scanning and automated updates
-   Set up intrusion detection systems
-   Monitor for unusual access patterns and data access
-   Create incident response procedures

## Guardrails that actually work in vibe-coding workflows {#guardrails-that-actually-work-in-vibe-coding-workflows .group .font-sans .text-[2em] .font-bold .text-black .dark:text-gray-100 .mt-12 .mb-3 .relative .scroll-mt-24}

-   **Vidoc Security Lab**: Automatically detect the vulnerability patterns covered in this guide-from hardcoded secrets and broken authorization to SQL injection and supply-chain risks. [**Try Vidoc Security Lab for free**](https://www.vidocsecurity.com){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white} and secure your code at the speed of vibe coding.
-   **Ownership and mandatory review for risky areas**: Auth flows, payments, and data-handling code should never merge on AI output alone. Require domain owners for approval ([Software Mind](https://softwaremind.com/blog/the-rise-and-risk-of-vibe-coding-whats-worth-knowing/ "The Pros and Cons of Vibe Coding"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **Secret hygiene**: Organization-wide secret scanning (repos, wikis, tickets, chat), automatic revocation/rotation SLAs, and pre-commit hooks ([GitGuardian](https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2025/ "The State of Secrets Sprawl 2025"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **Dependency policy**: Allowlist new packages, require integrity (hashes/signatures), prefer verified publishers, monitor SBOM for drift, and alert on trust changes ([TechRadar](https://www.techradar.com/pro/mitigating-the-risks-of-package-hallucination-and-slopsquatting "Mitigating the risks of package hallucination and 'slopsquatting' | TechRadar"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [SecurityWeek](https://www.securityweek.com/vibe-coding-when-everyones-a-developer-who-secures-the-code/ "Vibe Coding: When Everyone’s a Developer, Who Secures the Code? - SecurityWeek"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
-   **Security step in the vibe loop**: Treat AI output like a junior\'s draft-write tests, fuzz critical inputs, validate threat models, and only then deploy ([Google Cloud](https://cloud.google.com/discover/what-is-vibe-coding "Vibe Coding Explained: Tools and Guides | Google Cloud"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}, [IBM](https://www.ibm.com/think/topics/vibe-coding "What is Vibe Coding? | IBM"){.border-b .text-blue-700 .border-gray-300 .transition-[border-color] .hover:border-blue-600 .dark:text-white .dark:border-gray-500 .dark:hover:border-white}).
